### Page 2

足元の経済・金融状況と注目点
1
（出所） みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
2026. 11.17
米国では物価高で実質賃金が伸び悩む一方、
カード延滞率に改善の兆し。NY連銀によれば、
延滞率の高止まりはコロナ期の与信拡大が影響
しており、今後も緩やかな改善が続くとの見立て
米家計のクレジットカード延滞率に改善の兆し
米国
10月の消費者物価は市場予想を上回る結果。生産
者物価も高止まり。市場は12月利上げを織り込み。
一方で、金利上昇を背景に中古住宅販売は大幅減。
根強いインフレ圧力と住宅市場の弱さが鮮明に
欧州
ユーロ圏の7～9月期GDPは前期から減速。ドイツ
は小幅プラスも、フランスはマイナス、スペインは堅
調と主要国でばらつき。ユーロ圏全体では雇用の伸
びも横ばいとなっており、景気回復力の鈍さを確認
中国
10月の輸出は高い伸びを維持。半導体や自動車な
どがけん引。一方で輸入も大幅増。生産は輸出堅調
で底堅く推移も、投資は下落幅が拡大し、消費も力
強さ欠き低迷。消費者物価も前月から伸びが鈍化

日本
9月の実質消費支出は10カ月連続のマイナス。景気
ウォッチャー調査も6カ月ぶりに悪化し、家計部門の
弱さを示唆する内容。国内企業物価は原油高などを
受けて再び加速。今後消費者物価へ波及する公算
注目点①
日本の消費は所得の伸びを下回る緩慢な回復。
一方、大型M&Aなどから企業の資金需要は旺盛
で、銀行貸出は高い伸びを維持。預金獲得競争
が激化するなか、安定的な預金調達が重要に
日本の家計は消費に慎重、企業は借入に積極的
注目点②

米国：物価高で実質賃金は伸び悩むも、クレジットカード延滞率に改善の兆し
 2026年10月のコアCPI（食品・エネルギーを除く）は前月比＋0.3％と市場予想（同＋0.2％）を上回る結果
―3カ月移動平均では2025年1月以来の高水準。財・サービス価格ともに上昇が続きインフレ圧力の根強さを確認
― 名目賃金の増加が物価上昇に相殺され、実質平均時給は前月比横ばい。家計の購買力は伸び悩み
 一方、クレジットカードの90日以上延滞への新規移行率は高水準ながらも小幅に低下
―7～9月期は6.87％と前年同期の7.05％を下回り、家計の信用状況には改善の兆し
3
（出所）米国労働省より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

米国：カード延滞率の高さは足元の所得環境だけではなく、過去の与信拡大の影響を反映
 NY連銀によれば、コロナ禍には信用力の低い借り手へのカード発行が拡大
― 全体の信用スコアが改善する中、新規発行時の信用スコア中央値は低下。リスクの高い層への与信を増やした格好
 当時発行されたカードがその後の延滞率上昇に大きく寄与
― ただし、コロナ期の与信拡大による影響は徐々にはく落。加えて、2023年以降は新規発行時の信用力が改善しており、
雇用・所得環境が大きく悪化しなければ、今後も延滞率の緩やかな改善が続く公算
4

日本：個人消費は所得の伸びを下回る緩慢な拡大。先行き不安が回復の重石に
5
 個人消費は拡大基調を維持も、所得の伸びを下回る緩慢な拡大
―9月の消費活動指数は前年比＋0.8％と拡大基調を維持したものの、耐久財特需のはく落などから増勢は鈍化
― 実質雇用者報酬が着実に増加する一方、消費の伸びは所得を下回る状況が継続
 先行き不安も家計の慎重な支出姿勢に影響
― 消費者マインドは食品やエネルギーなど身近な消費財の価格上昇に対する懸念から低調に推移
― 中東情勢を巡る不透明感もあり、当面の消費は緩慢な拡大にとどまる見通し

日本：大型M&Aなどもあり銀行貸出は高い伸び。金利上昇下でも企業の資金需要は底堅く推移
6
 企業の資金需要は旺盛。銀行貸出は高い伸びを維持
―10月の貸出残高（銀行・信用金庫計）は前年比＋5.2％と都市銀行を中心に高い伸び。とりわけ、大企業の資金需要が
旺盛。中堅・中小企業の資金需要にも底堅さ。活発な企業買収も資金需要の追い風に
 金利上昇下でも当面の資金需要は底堅い見通し
― 日銀の利上げに伴い新規貸出金利は上昇も、投資需要などを背景に企業の借入需要は底堅く推移する見込み
― なお、地域銀行を中心に預貸率が上昇。メガバンクを含め預金獲得競争が激化するなか、安定的な預金調達が重要に

（電力価格、2026年1～8月累積、前年同期比、％）
米国：民主党勝利によるデータセンター規制の強化は電力価格の上昇を抑制する可能性
 米国では家庭向け電力価格が上昇。背景に送配電設備の更新・増強や異常気象
― 足元では、データセンター（DC）稼働数と電力価格の上昇率に明確な関係はみられず
 一方、DC増設に伴う将来の電気料金負担増や水資源への影響を懸念し、規制強化を求める動きが拡大
― 中間選挙では、DC規制の強化を掲げる民主党知事候補が各州で勝利。とりわけ、DCの集積が進むテキサス・オハイ
オ・ジョージアでは、長年共和党が知事職を握ってきたものの、民主党候補が勝利する結果。今後、DC開発を巡る規制
が強化され、家計への費用転嫁が抑えられれば、中長期的な電力価格の上昇ペースは緩和される可能性
7
（注） 青色はオハイオ州、テキサス州、ジョージア州

主な方針州・当選者
条件を満たさないDCの建設を停止。 事業者
に電気・ガス・水道の費用を全額負担させ、
労働組合に加入する労働者による建設、情
報公開、環境基準の遵守などを義務付ける
方針
オハイオ
エイミー・ア
クトン氏
新規DCの承認を一時停止。 住民が建設の
是非に関与できる法律が整うまで、新規案件
を認めない。DC向けの税優遇を廃止し、送
電網の増強費用などを一般家庭に転嫁させ
ない方針
テキサス
ジーナ・ヒノ
ホサ氏
州全体で新規DC建設を一時停止。 電気料
金や水資源への影響を調査し、建設ルール
を整備。DC向けの税優遇を廃止し、電力設
備の整備費用を事業者に負担させる方針

欧州：ユーロ圏経済はプラス成長継続。一方、フランスでは不安定な政治情勢が投資の重石に
 ユーロ圏全体の7～9月期GDPは潜在成長率（約＋1.2％）を上回る伸びを維持
― アイルランドを除くと年率＋0.8％成長。スペインは内需主導で高成長維持も、フランスがマイナス転化する等国ごとに濃淡
 フランスの不安定な政治情勢が景気に暗雲。設備投資の下押し要因に
― フランスでは不安定な政治情勢が経済の不確実性を高めるなか、民間設備投資の足踏みが続く
― 同国の10月企業景況感は前月から大幅に悪化し、縮小圏に転落。先行きも、政治・財政の不透明感の長期化や金融環境
の引き締まりを背景に、一部業種（情報通信サービス等）を除き低調に推移する見込み
8

中国：投資は下落幅が拡大し、消費も力強さを欠き低迷。生産は輸出堅調で底堅く推移

― 生産は輸出堅調で底堅く推移（前月：同＋5.5％⇒10月：同＋4.8％）。自動車が同＋0.9％と、内需低迷で伸びず

― 先行きは、信用スプレッド拡大など金融環境のタイト化を通じたAI投資の選別・減速が、アジアの輸出拡大に波及するリ

### Page 14

日本：7～9月期実質GDPは横ばい圏の推移。消費低調の一方、設備投資が堅調に回復
 2026年7～9月期の実質GDP（1次速報値）は前期比▲0.0％（年率▲0.1％）とほぼ横ばい
― 個人消費が2四半期連続でマイナスとなったほか、輸出が一時的要因もあり小幅減となり、成長率を下押し。一方、設備
投資はAI関連需要の堅調さを背景にプラス転換し、成長率を押し上げ
―4～6月期（前期比＋0.4％）から減速し、経済の緩やかな拡大傾向が一服
 今後、2026年度後半の実質GDPは、均してみれば小幅なプラス成長と予想
― 個人消費が精彩を欠く一方で、設備投資の拡大が支えになる構図が継続。2026年度全体では＋1％弱の成長率に

## Document 045: 20261106【投研報告】市場快訊_資料中心監管疑慮衝擊AI供應鏈，美股全面重挫

Source PDF: all report pdf/20261106【投研報告】市場快訊_資料中心監管疑慮衝擊AI供應鏈，美股全面重挫.pdf

Date: 2026-11-06
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

不另行通知。
資料中心監管疑慮衝擊 AI 供應鏈，美股全面重挫
2026 年 11 月 06 日
一、 市場動態
1. 資料中心監管疑慮引爆 AI 供應鏈賣壓，美股選後漲勢一日反轉：民主黨取得參、眾兩
院多數，美國進入分裂政府格局。S&P 500 於 11 月 4 日先漲 1.58%，11 月 5 日卻重
挫 4.36%，創 2025 年 4 月以來最大單日跌幅；Nasdaq 跌 5.52%，費半大跌 12.25%，
十一大產業全數收黑。主因有三：一是資料中心開發成為州級政策議題，德州州長當
選人主張暫停新建，全美新增容量約三成可能受影響；二是高通與 Arm 財報不如預

期；三是建設遞延將推高依賴外部融資業者的持有成本。費半重挫前已自 6 月高點回
落近三成，政策不確定性進一步放大跌幅（詳見表一）。
表一：美股主要指數表現（2026/11/05 收盤）
主要指數 收盤點位 單日漲跌幅
S&P 500 指數 7,353.73 −4.36%
Nasdaq 綜合指數 24,315.00 −5.52%
費城半導體指數 9,011.14 −12.25%
資料來源：Reuters、Nasdaq、元大銀行整理
2. 波動率指數急升，公債殖利率全面走低，油價反彈：11 月 5 日避險情緒升溫，VIX 盤
中升至 28.09（前日 16.61）。資金湧入美債，2 年、5 年、10 年及 30 年期殖利率分
別降至 4.46%、4.59%、4.84%及 5.25%，呈牛市陡峭化；12 月升息一碼機率由約
83%降至約 65%。布蘭特原油反彈 3.88%至每桶 98.29 美元，通膨前景仍具不確定性
（詳見表二）。
表二：美債殖利率與避險資產變化（2026/11/05）

2 年期公債殖利率 4.46% −12 bps
10 年期公債殖利率 4.84% −6 bps
VIX 波動率指數（盤中高點） 28.09 前日收盤 16.61
布蘭特原油期貨（美元／桶） 98.29 +3.88%
12 月升息一碼機率（CME） 約 65% 前日約 83%
資料來源：Bloomberg、WSJ／CME、元大銀行整理
二、 下跌原因
1. 民主黨橫掃國會及州長選舉，資料中心開發成為州級政策議題：德州州長當選人 Gina
Hinojosa 主張暫停新建資料中心，俄亥俄州與喬治亞州當選人也提出限制。地方政府

可透過電價分攤、建設許可及稅務優惠影響新案進度，UBS 估計 2028 年全美新增資
料中心容量約三成可能受影響。國會對科技巨頭與資料中心用電的監督亦可能增強，
半導體、電力設備及資料中心類股成為賣壓重心。
2. 高通手機晶片業績低於預期，加重半導體類股賣壓：高通財年第四季調整後 EPS 年減
28%至 2.17 美元，手機晶片營收年減 26%至 51.37 億美元，低於預期，主因蘋果相
關收入減少；汽車與物聯網營收雖年增 36%，仍難抵銷缺口，股價隔日收跌 9.5%，
進一步放大半導體跌幅。
3. 融資成本與投資回收壓力加重，依賴外部融資業者首當其衝：資料中心資本支出多發

生於上線前，若電力、許可或工程延後，收入認列隨之推遲，利息與持有成本卻須持
續負擔。儘管 12 月升息機率降至約 65%，借款成本仍高，依賴外部融資或單一客戶
合約的 AI 基礎建設業者將面臨回報壓縮與再融資風險。
4. 科技股在本次重挫前已明顯修正，評價落至極低水準：費半自 6 月 22 日高點 14,634.72
點已回落逾 30%，Nasdaq 亦自 10 月 6 日高點 27,599.89 點回落逾 10%。市場質疑
AI 投資效益，降低對高評價企業的容忍度，政策不確定性因而放大修正。近期跌幅較
屬估值下調而非基本面惡化，若晶片訂單與獲利預估維持穩定，市場有望回溫。

三、 市場看法
1. 短期評價修正而非長期趨勢反轉，AI 資本循環仍處擴張階段：我們視本波為多頭格局
中的健康修正。修正反映股東對龐大資本承諾要求更高風險補償，而非 AI 變現失速：
Google Cloud 第三季營收年增 103%、營業利益年增 209%，四大雲端業者資本支出
預估上修至約 1.16 兆美元，顯示企業看見更大市場而主動加碼（詳見表四）。此外，
政策衝擊集中於州與地方，聯邦仍支持資料中心擴建，選舉結果本質是要求用電成本
內部化，而非否定 AI 投資。
表四：AI 基本面持續擴張──關鍵指標一覽
指標 最新數據 解讀
Google Cloud 第三季營收 年增 103% AI 需求快速轉化為收入
Google Cloud 第三季營業利益 年增 209% 獲利能力未因擴張而惡化
四大雲端業者資本支出預估 約 8,732 億→1.16 兆美元 企業主動加碼，投資循環仍擴張
資料來源：元大投顧、Bloomberg；元大銀行整理

確性不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決
策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。 
2. 留意五大反彈訊號，利空逐步消化後市場可望回穩：（一）州級電價與併網制度若於
2027 年上半年建立，延後專案可望下半年重新加速，眾議院以 417 比 3 通過
《Ratepayer Protection Act》亦顯示跨黨派共識；（ 二）雲端業者資本支出指引未下
修、未見取消訂單；（ 三）11 月 10 日 CPI 及 13 日 PPI 物價月增率回落；（ 四）財政
部維持附息債標售規模，資金回流市場；（ 五）VIX 回落且投資等級債利差穩定。訊號
出現後，市場有望回歸基本面。 
3. 政策先影響建設速度，AI 需求仍須以訂單驗證：州級限制首先改變的是資料中心選址
與交付時程，而非 AI 需求本身。雲端客戶若維持支出計畫，專案可調整順序或轉往他
州，惟設備交付遞延將壓抑供應商近季營收；聯邦立法限制則須經國會及總統否決權，
短期落地難度高。若主要客戶訂單穩定，本次修正較屬建設時程遞延的風險重定價。 
4. 新增算力面臨遞延，既有算力相對有利：高盛估計可能收緊開發限制的州約占美國規
劃中資料中心容量的 45%，其中德州約占 25%。新案放慢下，大型雲端業者既有算力
可望受惠於利用率與議價能力提升，資本支出延後亦減輕現金流壓力；仍待併網的專
案及高槓桿業者則面臨回收遞延，產業鏈分化將擴大。 
5. 歷史經驗顯示選後股市多正向，後續表現仍宜留意基本面變化：彭博統計 1950 年以
來，美國期中選舉後 12 個月 S&P 500 平均報酬約 15%，19 次全數收正，最高為 1954
年的逾 30%，最弱的 1986 年亦小幅上漲。府會分治抑制政策大幅轉向為選後行情主
要支撐，惟後續表現仍須觀察基本面(詳見下圖)。

6. 企業獲利仍強勁成長，本次修正以估值與風險溢價重估為主：彭博 11 月 4 日統計，
S&P 500 已公布財報公司中 79.85%優於預期，資訊科技更達 92.45%，第三季 EPS
成長率分別為 29.0%及 67.8%（詳見表五）。 財報為美股提供中期支撐，短期評價則
取決於 2027 年獲利預估能否承受建設遞延與融資成本壓力。 
表五：S&P 500 第三季財報統計（截至 2026/11/04） 
類別 已公布家數 優於預期家數 優於預期比率 第三季 EPS 成長率

7. 最新經濟數據仍具韌性，聯準會利率路徑仍具變數：初領失業救濟金降至 18.9 萬人，
第三季勞動生產力季增年率 3.6%、單位勞動成本季減 0.7%，經濟仍具韌性。惟油價
逼近每桶 100 美元，若通膨高於預期，升息定價恐再升溫，壓抑科技股估值與長債價
格。
8. 信用市場是判斷風險是否由股權評價擴散至金融條件的重要指標：若 AI 企業持續擴大
資本支出與外部融資，償債能力將受關注。大型投資等級科技企業現金流穩定，較能

吸收壓力；高槓桿或依賴單一客戶的 AI 基礎建設業者則較易受利差上升衝擊。信用利
差若維持穩定，風險仍以股權評價調整為主；若同步擴大，則須警惕金融條件收緊。
四、 投資建議：
1. 短期修正不改 AI 長期趨勢，切勿恐慌殺低錯殺優質標的：本次重挫源自選後政策不確
定性與評價重新定價，AI 需求與企業獲利尚未反轉，屬多頭中的短期波動。應避免恐
慌出清而錯殺優質標的；已持有者以續抱為主，加碼宜分批逢低，避免高槓桿操作。
2. 以股債平衡穿越短期波動，等待基本面重新主導市場：我們認為，本次修正屬短期政
策衝擊與評價調整，中長期科技股展望仍正面。在政策與通膨明朗前，建議將集中度
過高的科技股部位，適度轉向波動較低的平衡型或多重資產商品，搭配優質債券穩定
收益，並保留股票部位分批布局優質標的，待反彈訊號確認後再提高風險資產比重。
3. 股票部位聚焦優質、評價回落且前景較佳的產業：修正後部分優質企業評價已回到合
理區間，股票建議聚焦：一、握有長期電力合約、已通電園區的大型雲端業者；二、
明年展望佳的 AI 供應鏈，如 ASIC、光通訊、電力設備、半導體設備及資安；三、跌

幅較小的軟體及非科技優質股。高度仰賴新建資料中心與外部融資的業者，宜待環境
明朗再評估。
4. 中天期美國公債及高信評投資等級債仍較具優勢：在美國政策風險升溫及科技股評價
調整期間，美國公債仍具投資組合避險功能，但考量通膨與能源價格的不確定性，現
階段不建議延長存續期間。中天期美國公債及高信評投資等級債在兼顧票息收益、利
率敏感度與資產配置穩定性上仍較具優勢。信用債方面，營運現金流充足、槓桿適中
及再融資需求可控的發行人仍較為穩健，對 AI 相關企業則須進一步評估資本支出、合
約現金流與債務到期結構。

2  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
⚫ 美國聯準會如市場預期，維持政策利率不變。決策官員仍對通膨保持擔憂，也承
認美國經濟正以「強勁速度」成長。聯準會主席 Kevin Warsh 在會後談話中，並
未明確排除進一步緊縮政策的可能，激勵市場對 12 月升息的預期升溫。數據方
面，美國第三季 GDP 年化成長率達 3.4%，高於前值的 2.2%，亦優於市場預期
的 3.2%，印證美國經濟動能強勁。此外，9 月 PCE 與核心 PCE 符合預期，但凸
顯通膨壓力持續偏高。在「通膨高企」與「經濟強韌」雙重因素驅動下，市場預
期聯準會將於 12 月升息，為美元指數提供有力支撐。惟近期受 AI 產業股票波
動加劇影響，部分資金轉入美債避險，導致美元指數在高檔面臨調節性賣壓。
⚫ 儘管受能源價格走揚影響，9 月歐元區通膨率升至 3.8%，但在薪資壓力維持溫
和，且歐洲央行尚未觀察到第二輪通膨效應全面擴散的情況下，歐洲央行上週決
議維持政策利率不變。通膨回升令市場預期歐洲央行將在下次會議升息，為歐元

提供部分支撐，但考量歐元區經濟成長表現仍落後美國，在美元基本面強勁的抑
制下，歐元反彈力道相對有限。目前歐元最大的潛在風險在於部分國家的債券市
場壓力尚未消散，市場對歐洲財政風險仍保持高度警戒。
⚫ 日本央行維持政策利率於 1.25%不變，並且首次明確表示已達成其 2%的通膨目
標；過去日本央行雖認為通膨改善，但始終強調尚未穩定達標，如今首次正式承
認達成 2%目標，代表未來升息門檻進一步下降。此外，日本央行將經濟成長預
測維持在 0.7%至 0.8%，並小幅上修 CPI 預測，令市場預期日央 12 月有望再次
升息，日本貨幣政策正常化將有助日圓結束疲軟走勢。

3  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
 美元兌新台幣匯率走勢  
Nov/2/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週台幣匯價高點為 32.390，低點在 32.676，以 32.478 作收，較前一周收盤價
升值 3 分。 
上週台幣先升後貶，周初，市場等待聯準會利率決議，交投偏謹慎觀望，而外
資在前周大幅匯出之後，轉為雙向進出，台幣止跌回穩，最高來到 32.390 的周內高
點；然而周間，美國科技股財報表現失色，台股賣壓沉重，外資賣超台股並再度轉
為大幅匯出，台幣走貶，最低來到 32.676，不過匯價逢高吸引出口商賣壓出籠，加
上央行提供流動性，台幣貶幅收斂，終場在央行調節之下收在 32.478。

＜未來分析＞ 
    預估本週台幣偏弱震盪。就國際市場來看，聯準會 10 月利率決策維持利率不
變，但有兩名官員主張升息 1 碼，且聯準會表示通膨仍處於高檔，顯示聯準會對通
膨仍保持警戒，市場預期美國利率將維持高檔，台美利差仍維持在相對高位，不利
台幣表現；另一方面，美國科技股財報表現不如預期，引發市場對 AI 投資回報的疑
慮，對台股也帶來壓力。就台灣市場來看，外資持續調節台股並大幅匯出，10 月累
計賣超將近兆元，資金匯出壓力大，不過一方面央行持續進場調節，加上匯價逢高
預計吸引出口商進場拋匯，料將限制台幣貶值速度。綜合以上，預估本週台幣走勢
偏弱。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：32.300-32.800

美元兌人民幣匯率走勢  
Nov/2/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
上週人民幣持續走升。周初市場等待美國聯準會利率決議，交投偏向觀望，境
外人民幣主要於 6.6600 上方震盪；周間聯準會如預期維持利率不變，美元指數自 102
上方回落，加上中國出口結匯需求持續提供支撐，境外人民幣突破 6.6600 整數關口；
週五人行中間價續創近三年九個月新高，最高來到 6.7138，顯示中國人行允許人民
幣溫和升值的走勢，境外人民幣一度升破 6.6500 整數關口，最高來到 6.6487，終場
收在 6.6528，週線升值 162 點，境內人民幣則收在 6.6558，週線升值 146 點。

＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週人民幣偏升。就中國國內市場來看，10 月官方製造業 PMI 降至 49.3，
重新落入景氣收縮區間，反映中國內需及製造業動能仍有待改善，不過 9 月出口年
增 36.3%，出口商結匯需求持續提供人民幣支撐，加上人行中間價續創近三年九個
月新高，預計將持續帶動人民幣走升；就國際市場來看，聯準會 10 月利率決策維持
利率不變，但有兩名官員主張升息 1 碼，顯示聯準會對通膨仍保持警戒，市場預期
美國利率將維持高檔，美中利差維持在相對高位，將限制人民幣升值的速度與幅度；
最後就技術面來看，USD/CNH 日線圖中，各天期均線向下發散，匯價維持在五日均
線之下交投，均線呈現空頭排列，顯示空方勢力較盛，綜合以上，預估本週人民幣
偏升。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌人民幣：6.6200-6.6700

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週 USD/JPY 上漲。週初，美伊衝突仍陷僵局支撐美元避險買盤，USD/JPY 最
高來到 158.34；週間，聯準會公布利率維持利率不變，USD/JPY 持續在 158 附近盤
整；臨近週末，日本央行公布利率維持不變並釋放偏鷹訊號，USD/JPY 最低來到
156.94，終場收在 157.53，週線上漲 72 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 USD/JPY 區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、日本央行於上周五宣布利
率維持在 1.25%不變，並表示隨著基礎通膨率大致達到 2%，央行必須持續控制通
膨穩定於此目標，且再次強調貨幣政策已進入更為緊縮階段，未來每隔數月即可能
升息。此外，日本央行在季度報告中，亦將截至 2028 年 3 月財年的核心消費者通
膨預估自三個月前預測的 2.4%上調至 2.6%。日本央行偏鷹立場令市場預期日本最
快將於 12 月再次升息，預計將有利日圓表現。二、聯準會宣布維持利率於 3.75%-
4%區間不變，惟因通膨仍處高位，聯準會重申將致力實現 2%的長期通膨區間， 
FedWatch 顯示 12 月升息機率約七成，美元持穩，預計將限縮 USD/JPY 下行空
間。三、就技術面而言，在日線圖中，USD/JPY 各天期均線趨平，近期匯價大致
在 156 至 158 間震盪，匯價暫無明確方向性。綜合以上，預估 USD/JPY 本週呈現
區間震盪的可能性較高。

美元兌歐元匯率走勢

＜上週走勢＞ 
  上週 EUR/USD 下跌。週初，美伊衝突持續以及法國政經情勢動盪等情況下，
歐元走跌，最低來到 1.1111；週間，聯準會和歐洲央行相繼公布利率決議均維持不
變，歐洲央行發表言論偏鷹派，歐元止跌回升至 1.1214；臨近週末，歐元區公布經
濟數據好壞參半，歐元自高點回落，終場收在 1.1158，週線下跌 41 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 EUR/USD 區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、歐洲央行如市場預期宣布
存款利率維持在 2.5%不變，並警告能源成本持續上漲將對通膨造成上行風險，暗示
可能在 12 月再次升息，升息預期升溫預計將支撐歐元走勢。二、歐元區第三季 GDP
年增 1.3%，高於市場預期的 1.1%，顯示在能源價格持續上漲的不利因素下，經濟仍
展現韌性，預計有利歐元表現。三、聯準會宣布維持利率在 3.75%-4%不變，並重申
致力控制通膨的立場。此外，美國公布第三季 GDP 成長 3.4%，優於市場預期的 3.2%，
9 月核心 PCE 年增率自前月的 3%上升至 3.1%，在美國經濟強勁及通膨仍高的情況
下，市場預期聯準會將於 12 月升息一碼，美元持穩，預計將限縮歐元走升空間。四、
技術面而言，在日線圖中，EUR/USD 各天期均線向下發散，不過近期匯價在多次向
下測試 1.1120 附近均未有效跌破，顯示下方仍有買盤支撐。綜合以上，預估 EUR/USD
本週呈現區間震盪的可能性較高。

5 年期指標券 A15108 議價成交在 1.845%~1.846%。 
美債利率自波段高點回落，且央行總裁楊金龍本週立法院發言維持近來對通
膨容忍度變寬之態度，惟市場對央行升息預期並未完全消除，買盤追價動力有
限，預期台債殖利率維持區間整理，關注下週 30 年期公債標售果，預估 5 年
券區間為 1.80%~1.85%，10 年券區間為 1.99%~2.04%。

### Page 1

M
Idea
Alchip Technologies Ltd  | Asia Pacific
European NDR: strong five-year
outlook in AI ASICs; OW
Alchip's strong growth potential is rooted in US CSPs' AI ASIC
replacement cycle. Even after the recent rally, our price target
still implies 46% upside.
Right after its  3Q26 earnings call, we took Alchip's CEO, Mr. Johnny Shen, and CFO,
Mr. Daniel Wang, to visit European investors for discussion of the company’s long-
term strategy and outlook. Here are key takeaways:

Entering another phase of fast growth in the coming 4-5 years, with good
profitability: Alchip is sticking with its back-end design service focus, which
dovetails with CSPs' strategy of COT (customer-owned tooling). The long-term
trend is for CSP and AI lab customers to own the architecture and front-end design,
while pursuing efficiency in back-end design and production service. That strategy
can maximize the tokens generated per dollar  of AI capex. Besides winning future

generations with a major customer (AWS, 2nm and 1.4nm), Alchip has secured a
second big customer and is very close to winning a project with another US CSP (we
think it should be Google –  TPU computing dies). We project US$10bn total revenue
in 2028 –  4x our US$2.4bn estimate for  2026. Management thinks US$20bn
revenue p.a. by the end of this decade is possible, if the balance sheet permits.
More discussion of the second customer: The company still cannot disclose the

name of the second customer given the NDA, but reiterated that it expects a US$1bn
revenue contribution in 2027. Alchip provides part of the design service for the end
customer, and the gross margin is reasonably good. Although we don’t rule out the
possibility of another design service provider in this ASIC project for IP or front-end
design, we maintain our view that the end customer is a US CSP that uses Alchip for
back-end design service. Our industry checks don't see a CoWoS booking for this

ASIC, so we think it could be related to AI networking.
Getting close to winning a third big customer: In our October 11 report, Solid 2027
and 2028 outlook with potential catalysts, we already indicated that Alchip is a
strong candidate for Google TPU computing die back-end design service.
Management continued to show high confidence in a new project win without
specifying customer name; it expects some NRE revenue to come by the end of
2027. We asked which customer (second or third) could make a bigger revenue

contribution 2-3 years down the road – management thinks the third customer may
show bigger revenue potential.
Alchip's value underpinned by major customer (AWS): This could be reflected in
higher allocation of Trainium3 in 2027 and better business terms for Trainium4
(profits to grow with the volume upside). Management also expects 1.4nm design
activities to kick off some time in 2027 –  the key to five-year growth visibility.
Please see the following section for investors' feedback and our views.

### Page 2

Idea
2
Stronger revenue   guidance for 2027 and 2028
European investors' feedback
1. Alchip was somewhat overlooked in the AI ASIC market over the past two years,
compared with MediaTek and GUC, which continued to win new projects. Winning
second and third big customers is considered a re-rating driver – it mitigates the
concentration in a single customer (AWS).
2.  Meanwhile, European investors generally agree that AI ASICs should gain share
against GPUs, given tighter AI capex budgets. We share the view expressed in

recent comments from Alchip and MediaTek management: certain AI ASICs that
companies help to design provide very competitive TCO (total cost of ownership).
3. It is hard to track ASIC supply chain data points in real time, but the ASIC story
and Alchip's industry position can justify a long-term investment. Changes in
short-term data points wouldn't stop European investors from investing in Alchip.
4.  On valuation, investors think Alchip's 2028e implied P/E of 11x based on our EPS

estimate is relatively attractive vs. other global ASIC design service peers.
Considering this feedback, we think Alchip stock should  continue to re-rate: The stock
has been volatile, rebounding 50% since November 6 after a 40% pullback over October
12 to November  6. (The TAIEX was  +10% and -20% over those periods.) Based on our
estimates, P/E is now 17x for 2027 and 11x for 2028, lower than its historical average of
28x one-year forward P/E since 2019.

Key catalysts could be:
• November 30 –  AWS re:Invent conference with Trainium4 announcement;
• Supply chain identification of the second big customer's name and order potential;
• Confirmation of Google computing die back-end design service;
• Bigger Trainium3 revenue ramp in 3Q27 vs. 2Q27.

number of use cases for Trainium (e.g.,

revenue growth in 2026.

AI chipsets and HPC projects start to

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

## Document 225: [000660] SK하이닉스(000660) 여전히 강력하다SK하이닉스(000660) 여전히 강력하다여전히 강력하다 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[000660] SK하이닉스(000660) 여전히 강력하다SK하이닉스(000660) 여전히 강력하다여전히 강력하다 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-29
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

00

SK 하이닉스 (000660/KS) 
여전히 강력하다 
3Q26 영업이익 83 조원으로 예상 상회 
SK하이닉스의 3Q26 영업이익은 83조원 (+38% QoQ)으로 예상을 상회했다. 메모
리 공급 부족 심화, HBM4 반영 시작에 따른 메모리 가격 상승이 환율 하락을 상쇄했
기 때문이다. 출하 DRAM +10%, NAND -1%, 가격 DRAM +28%, NAND 
+24%, 영업이익률 DRAM 82%, NAND 72%, HBM 판가는 HBM4 출하가 시작되
며 37% 수준 상승한 것으로 추정된다. 업황은 여전히 예상을 상회하고 있다.

4Q26 분기 영업이익 100 조원 시대 진입 전망 
4Q26 영업이익은 105조원 (+27% QoQ)으로 분기 100조원 시대에 진입할 것으로 
전망한다. 메모리 가격 강세가 지속되는 가운데, 1c 출하 가속화, M15X 램프업 본격화 
등으로 출하량 상승이 기존 예상을 상회할 것이다. 또한 HBM4 의 Cross-over 시작 
및 수율 안정화로 HBM 가격, 수익성 제고 동반이 지속될 것이다. 출하는 DRAM 
+24%, NAND +4%, 가격은 DRAM +8%, NAND +7%를 예상한다.

2027 년 영업이익 488 조원 (+70% YoY)으로 상향조정 
SK 하이닉스의 2027년 영업이익을 488 조원 (+70% YoY)으로 12% 상향 조정한
다. 원/달러 환율 전망치를 기존 1,340 원에서 1,315 원으로 하향 조정했음에도, 공급 
부족 심화 속 2027년향 HBM 가격 인상 및 예상을 상회하는 수율, 1Q27 진행될 모바
일 DRAM 가격 인상 등을 반영했기 때문이다. LTA 가격 역시 개별 약정된 범위 내 
시장 상황을 반영할 수 있는 구조라는 점을 감안하면, 지속 상승할 것이라 전망한다.

높은 주주환원 의지 확인, 높아진 실적 가시성, 지나친 저평가 
투자의견 매수, 목표주가 4,000,000원을 유지한다. SK하이닉스는 LTA에 대해 기
존 5년에서 추가 연장에 대한 논의가 진행 중이며, 2029년까지 HBM, DRAM은 사실
상 완판, HBM 수요는 공급 가능 물량의 2 배를 상회, 2030 년까지 메모리 수요 우위 
국면 전망을 밝혔다. AI 인프라 투자의 강력한 명분과 메모리 수요의 구조성, 잠재적으
로 매우 높은 LTA 위반 비용을 감안하면 실적 가시성과 안정성 제고가 동반되는 국면
이다. 100 조원 수준의 주주환원은 2026 년 예상 FCF 의 66% 수준, 분기별 자사주 
매입, 소각 지속 계획을 통해 높은 주주환원 의지를 보여주고 있다. 실적 Duration 장
기화, 주주환원을 감안하면 12MF P/E 3.4X 수준의 현 주가는 현저한 저평가 국면이
다. 과도한 우려의 되돌림과 메모리 밸류에 대한 재논의 국면 진입을 전망한다.

2026-10-29

반도체. 한동희 / donghee.han@sks.co.kr / 3773-8826

## Document 249: [034020] 두산에너빌리티(034020) 펀더멘털은 견고, 노이즈로 인한 일시적 주가 하락두산에너빌리티(034020) 펀더멘털은 견고, 노이즈로 인한_07bab1f0

Source PDF: all report pdf/[034020] 두산에너빌리티(034020) 펀더멘털은 견고, 노이즈로 인한 일시적 주가 하락두산에너빌리티(034020) 펀더멘털은 견고, 노이즈로 인한_07bab1f0.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

(034020) 
두산에너빌리티 
2026.10.30

에너지/인프라/배터리 황성현, Ph.D. 
6878 / tjdgus2009@eugenefn.com

펀더멘털은 견고, 노이즈로 인한 일시적 주가 하락 
 투자의견 BUY(유지) 
 목표주가 80,000 원(하향) 
 현재주가 56,700 원(10/29)

 [투자의견] 당사는 두산에너빌리티에 대해 목표주가를 80,000원(2027년 
BPS의 6.2배)으로 27% 하향하며, 투자의견 BUY를 유지한다. 실적은 견조
한 회복을 보이고 있으나, 최근 GE Vernova(GEV) 등 글로벌 AI 및 에너지 
밸류체인 Peer 주가 조정을 반영해 멀티플을 하향 조정했다. 체코 시공 계
약 등 일부 노이즈에 의해 주가가 급락한 만큼 매수 기회라고 판단한다.  
 [3Q26 Review] 매출액 4.4조원(-5.8%qoq, +14.7%yoy), 영업이익 
2,612억원(-16.9%qoq, +90.6%yoy)을 기록해 당사 추정에 부합하는 무
난한 실적을 기록했다. 에너빌리티 부문은 매출액 1.8조원, 영업이익 1,211
억원(영업이익률 6.6%, +2.3%p qoq)을 기록하며 원자력·가스터빈 중심의 
고수익 사업 믹스 개선으로 분기 수익성이 재차 개선됐다. 당기순이익은 
149억원(-93.4%qoq)에 그쳤으나, 원달러 환율 급락으로 발생한 환손실 
반영 때문으로 이익 체력과는 무관하다고 판단한다. 3Q26 미국 빅테크 데
이터센터향 가스터빈 첫 출하를 달성했으며, 수주잔고는 에너빌리티 부문 
기준 26.6조원(+70.9%yoy)으로 사상 최대치를 경신했다. 
 [4Q26 Preview] 매출액 4.8조원, 영업이익 2,640억원으로 유사한 실적 
흐름을 전망한다. 4Q 가스터빈 인도와 체코 두코바니 원전 진행률 반영으로 
에너빌리티 부문 외형 성장이 기대된다. 3Q26 별도 기준 영업이익률이 
8.5%까지 개선된 만큼 에너빌리티 부문의 분기 실적 개선 추세는 유효하다
고 판단한다. 연간 수주 목표 13.3조원 달성을 위한 잔여 수주(3.3조원 +α)
가 예정되어 있으며, 대형원전을 제외하더라도 가이던스 달성은 무난할 것
이라 판단한다. 세부적으로는 SMR 1조원(계약 조건 합의 완료 단계로 연내 
체결 가능), 신재생 7,000억원(해상풍력 기자재·EPC 2건 협의 완료 단계), 
해외 복합 EPC 5,000억원(국내 1건, 해외 2건)이 기대된다. 
 [가스터빈] 3Q26 누적 가스터빈 13기·스팀터빈 11기·LTSA 6건을 달성하
며 연간 10기 이상 수주·수행 역량이 안정적으로 유지되고 있다. 2030년 
슬롯 예약 물량의 본계약 전환은 2027년, 2031년 슬롯은 2028년으로 예
상되며, 수주잔고 증가가 기대된다. 가스터빈 수요 증가를 감안하면 추가 캐
파 증설 결정이 이르면 연내 이루어질 전망이다. 
 [원전] 10월 NuScale 1개 모듈 제작 계약을 체결했으며, PPA 지연 여부와 
무관하게 완결된 계약으로 실적에 반영될 전망이다. X-energy는 연내 기자
재 및 부품의 후속 계약을 추진 중이다. TerraPower 주기기 3종 중 2종 계
약이 완료되어 제작에 착수했으며, Rolls-Royce SMR·i-SMR 등 파트너십 
확대도 지속되고 있다. 이외에 대미투자 관련 기자재 발주는 미국 부지 확정 
및 한국 의회 승인 절차가 남아있어 내년 발주가 기대된다. 기존 웨스팅하우
스 AP1000 Vogtle 계약 대비 업무 범위 확대(원자로·증기발생기 외 추가 
품목) 가능성이 있어 긍정적으로 평가한다.

### Page 2

두산에너빌리티

2_ Eugene Research Center 
설명회 Q&A 
Q1. 4Q26 SMR 수주 목표 약 1 조원의 실현 가능성 
- 고객사와 GTC 등 주요 계약 조건에 대한 합의를 대부분 완료하고 계약 체결 대기 중 
- 연내 계약 가능성이 있어 수주 목표는 의미 있는 수준

Q2. NuScale 모듈 1 기 제작 계약의 PPA 지연 영향 
- 완결된 제작 계약으로 PPA 체결이 지연되더라도 해당 모듈의 제작 및 진행에는 문제없음

Q3. AP1000 장주기 기자재 선발주 후 인허가 지연 시 회계 처리 및 사업 영향 
- DOE·DOC 및 한국 정부 재원을 활용해 EPC 계약·착공 지연과 무관하게 기자재 제작 가능 
- 프로젝트 중단 시 웨스팅하우스의 다른 프로젝트로 기자재 전용 가능성 검토

Q4. 체코 원전 컨소시엄 변경 시 참여 및 사업 계획 
- 현지 지점 운영과 시공인력 상주로 시공 환경 자료 확보 
- 한수원과 초기 인허가·설계에 참여하고, 주기기 공급·설치 역량을 바탕으로 경쟁력 확보 기대

Q5. 체코 원전 EPC 사업의 수익성 확보 가능성 
- 한수원 가이드에 따라 수익성을 고려한 입찰 진행 예정

Q6. 웨스팅하우스 SPV 및 폴란드 AP1000 장주기 기자재 발주 진행 상황 
- DOE·DOC 프로젝트와 폴란드 AP1000 3 기 기자재 조기 발주 협의 중 
- 연내 수주 여부는 불확실하나 조기 수주 추진

Q7. 3Q26 영업이익률 개선 요인 
- 원자력·가스터빈 중심의 사업 믹스 개선이 수익성에 기여 
- 제작 진행률과 마일스톤에 따라 이익 인식

### Page 3

두산에너빌리티

Eugene Research Center _3 
Q8. 4Q26 복합화력 수주 목표 5,000 억원의 달성 가능성 
- 국내 1 건, 해외 2 건 협의 중이며 국내 프로젝트의 수주 가능성이 높은 상황 
- 대미 투자 물량은 기존 2031 년까지의 예약계약 납기와 비교해 검토 중

Q9. 3Q26 에너빌리티부문 당기순손실 원인 
- 급격한 환율 하락에 따른 약 800 억원의 환산손실이 주요 원인

Q10. 대미 투자 관련 기자재 발주 시점 
- 미국 부지 확정, 웨스팅하우스 사업제안 및 한국 정부 심사·국회 승인 절차 필요 
- 연말~내년 초 발주 여부는 불확실하나 조기 발주 추진

Q11. 대미 투자 프로젝트의 기자재 공급 범위 확대 가능성 
- 원자로·증기발생기 외 추가 품목까지 공급 범위 확대를 위해 웨스팅하우스와 협상 중

Q12. 장주기 기자재 선발주 후 인허가 지연 시 실적 영향 
- 기자재 출하 시 수금 조건이 충족되며 선발주 자금도 사전 확보 
- 보글·VC 서머 사례에서 기자재 대금을 회수해 프로젝트 지연에 따른 실적 영향은 제한적

Q13. 4Q26 신재생·풍력 수주 목표 약 7,000 억원의 달성 가능성 
- 해상풍력 기자재·EPC 계약 및 유지보수 계약 추진 중 
- 주요 계약 협의가 상당 부분 완료돼 일정 지연이 없다면 연내 목표 달성 전망

Q14. 중형 가스터빈 개발 및 해외 진출 계획 
- 대형 가스터빈은 한국·미국 중심으로 추진하며 중동 고객사와도 협의 중 
- 휴스턴 서비스 허브를 기반으로 미국 데이터센터 시장 대응 
- 중형 가스터빈은 시제품 제작 및 시험 진행 후 2028 년 하반기 출하 목표

### Page 4

두산에너빌리티

4_ Eugene Research Center 
Q15. NuScale 모듈 1 기 선발주 배경 
- 대규모 일괄 발주에 대비한 제작 경험 확보 및 납기 리스크 완화를 위해 사전 제작 추진

Q16. X-energy 연내 후속 계약 대상 품목 
- 증기발생기 전열관 및 주단 소재 등 장주기 품목의 사전 발주 추진

Q17. DOE 프로젝트의 장주기 기자재 공급 범위 확대 가능성 
- 웨스팅하우스 공급망 상황에 따라 추가 품목 협상 중 
- 과거 보글·VC 서머보다 넓은 공급 범위 기대

Q18. 웨스팅하우스 지분 투자와 기자재 계약 시점의 연계 가능성 
- 지분 투자는 내년, 기자재 계약은 연내 또는 내년 초 목표로 시점이 일치할지는 불확실

Q19. X-energy 장주기 기자재의 소재·부품 구분 
- 증기발생기 전열관은 부품, 주단 소재는 소재에 해당 
- 전열관은 공급 병목에 대비해 조기 발주하고 주단 소재는 설계 확정 후 제작 준비

Q20. 복합화력 주기기 수주의 기존·신규 고객 비중 
- 기존 고객과 추가 예약계약을 협의 중이며 신규 글로벌 고객과도 예약계약 협상 진행

Q21. 주요 SMR 사업 외 파트너십 확대 가능성 
- Rolls-Royce 사업 추진 및 GE Hitachi BWRX-300 주단 소재 예약계약 체결 
- i-SMR 은 2035 년 운전을 목표로 2028 년 이전 발주가 필요해 관련 협의 진행

Q22. X-energy 전열관·주단 소재 수주 물량 규모 
- 다우케미컬 초도 사업은 80MW 원자로 4 개 모듈로 구성되며, 해당 4 개 모듈분 부품·소재 공급

### Page 5

두산에너빌리티

Eugene Research Center _5 
Q23. 중동 지정학적 불안정에 따른 프로젝트·물류 영향 
- 기존 프로젝트의 운송비 증가분을 발주처·개발사와 협의 중 
- 신규 계약에는 비용 보상 및 공기 지연 관련 조항 반영

Q24. 가스터빈 생산능력 증설 규모 및 시기 
- 현재 생산능력 증설 중이며 2031 년 출하분까지 예약 접수 
- 시장 수요에 따라 추가 증설 검토

Q25. 2030 년 가스터빈 예약 물량의 실제 계약 전환 시점 
- 2030 년 출하 물량은 대부분 내년 실제 계약 여부 결정 전망 
- 2031 년 출하 물량은 2028 년경 계약 여부 결정 예상

## Document 279: AI関連投資の失速は日本にどう波及するか ～日銀の金融システムレポートから考察する三つの経路～

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI関連投資の失速は日本にどう波及するか ～日銀の金融システムレポートから考察する三つの経路～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-10-29
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Research Report

2026年10月 29日

1/8 
AI関連投資の失速は日本にどう波及するか 
～日銀の金融システムレポートから考察する三つの経路～ 
 資産運用調査部 主席研究員  奥田 宏二

（要旨） 
○ 日銀は 2026年 10月の金融システムレポートで、人工知能（AI）関連投資への期待が縮小し
た場合の金融安定上のリスクを分析した。本稿では同レポートに基づき日本へのリスクの波
及経路を整理したうえで、金融機関のストレス耐性を確認する。 
○ 米国の巨大IT企業による AI関連投資が急拡大している。これらの企業は従来の自己資金に
加え、社債発行などによる外部資金調達を増やしている。データセンター事業では、長期リ
ース契約を裏付けとした銀行融資や、プライベートファンドによる投融資が広がっている。
AI関連の旺盛な資金需要は経済成長につながる一方、特定分野へのリスクの集中と表裏一体
の関係にある。 
○ 日本への影響の波及経路として、銀行融資、金融市場、実体経済の三つが考えられる。日本
の大手行は海外のデータセンター事業者やファンドへの融資を増やしている。また、AI関連
株の下落をきっかけにヘッジファンドが投資ポジションを縮小すれば、日本の株式市場だけ
でなく、国債市場にも影響が及ぶ可能性がある。AI関連投資の減速によるアジア経済の悪化
も、日本の金融機関の信用コスト増加につながり得る。 
○ 日銀のストレステストでは、AI関連投資への期待の縮小とノンバンクによるショックの増幅
を想定しても、日本の金融機関は相応の耐性を備えているとの結果となった。一方、AI関連
企業の間では出資と将来の売上高の約束が結びついた循環的な取引がみられ、その影響はハ
イパースケーラーを通じて日本の大手行のデータセンター向け融資にも間接的に及び得る。
取引の連鎖全体を見る視点が必要だ。

1. はじめに 
日銀は10月23日、金融システムレポートを公表した。IMFが先に公表した国際金融安定性報告
書（GFSR）と同様、金融安定上のリスク要因として人工知能（AI）に焦点を当てた。AI関連企業
の成長期待を背景に、世界の株式市場では株価上昇が続き、データセンターなどへの設備投資も
拡大している。一方、投資規模が膨らむにつれて、巨額の投資に見合った収益が実現するかどう
かが金融市場の大きな関心事となっている。

### Page 2

2/8 
今回、注目したいのはAI関連投資と海外のノンバンク金融仲介機関（NBFI）の動向だ。AI関連
投資への期待が縮小した場合、海外ノンバンクの資産売却などを通じてショックが増幅され、日
本の金融市場にも波及する可能性を日銀は指摘している。本稿では、日銀の分析をもとに、AI関
連投資を支える資金調達の変化と、海外ノンバンクを通じた日本へのリスクの波及経路を整理し
たうえで、金融機関のストレス耐性を確認する。

2. 拡大するAI投資とノンバンクの役割 
AI関連投資は近年、大幅に増えている。日銀の図表によると、米国の巨大IT企業（ハイパース
ケーラー）5社の設備投資額は、2022年頃の四半期約 400億ドルから2026年 4～6月期には約
1,800億ドルと4倍以上に増加した（注1）。これらの企業は従来、潤沢な営業キャッシュフロー
をもとにデータセンター投資を行ってきた。ところが、足元では社債発行などによる外部資金調
達を増やすようになった。投資額の大きいデータセンターを例に見ていこう。 
ハイパースケーラーでは、自社でデータセンターを建設するだけでなく、外部事業者と長期リ
ース契約を結んで利用権を確保する動きも目立っている。5社の未開始のリース契約額は2026年
4～6月期に約1.1兆ドルに達した（資料 1、注1）。

資料 1 米ハイパースケーラー5社の設備投資と資金調達

（注）集計対象はAlphabet、Amazon、Meta、Microsoft、Oracleの 5社。①の「ネット借入・社債発行」と「ネット株式発行」は、プラス
が資金調達。直近は 2026年 4～6月期。数値は日本銀行の図表から筆者が読み取った概数。 
（出所）日本銀行「金融システムレポート」（2026年 10月号）図表Ⅴ-2-8（原資料はLSEG Workspace、SEC）をもとに第一ライフ資
産運用経済研究所作成

データセンターを建設・保有する事業者は、ハイパースケーラーとの長期リース契約を裏付け
に、銀行などから建設資金を調達している。銀行が融資を組成し、その貸出債権をプライベート
ファンドが購入する事例や、ファンドがデータセンター事業に直接出資する事例もある。こうし
て、AI関連投資を支える資金の出し手として、銀行に加えてプライベートファンドなどのNBFIの

### Page 3

3/8 
存在感が高まっている。 
日銀が集計した2023年1月から 2026年8月までに公表された米国のデータセンター案件で
は、資金調達額は合計約 1.6兆ドルに上る。このうち調達済みは約 0.2兆ドルにとどまり、約1.4
兆ドルが計画段階にある。また、案件数の約 2割でプライベートファンドがスポンサーとなって
いる（注2）。  
さらに、AI関連企業の間では、出資と将来の売上高が結びついた循環的な取引もみられる。IMF
が2026年10月に公表したGFSRによると、大手IT企業などが財務基盤の相対的に弱いAI企業に出
資し、出資を受けた企業が出資元などにサービスや半導体を発注する事例がある。IMFが確認した
2023年以降の14案件では、約 3,000億ドルの出資と約 6,500億ドルの債務調達が、約 1.4兆ドル
の将来の売上高の約束と結びついている。出資先の経営が悪化すれば、将来の売上高と投融資の
価値が同時に損なわれる可能性がある。 
こうした資金調達の拡大によって、AI関連投資と銀行、プライベートファンドなどの結びつき
が強まっている。AI関連投資が期待どおりの収益を生まなければ、データセンター事業者への融
資や出資を通じて、銀行やファンドに損失が生じる可能性がある。さらに、銀行はファンド自体
にも融資しており、ファンドの財務状況の悪化が銀行に波及する経路もある。日銀は、こうした
金融主体間の結びつきを通じて、ショックが金融システムに広がる可能性を警戒している。

3. 日本に及ぶ三つの波及経路 
AI関連投資の収益性に対する懸念の高まりや投資そのものの減速に直面した場合、日本の金融
システムにはどのような影響が及ぶのか。日銀の分析をもとに、①銀行融資、②金融市場、③実
体経済――の三つの波及経路からみていく。

（1） 銀行の融資を通じた経路 
日本の大手行は海外でデータセンター事業者やプライベートファンドへの融資を増やしてい
る。日銀によると、2026年 3月末時点で 3メガ行の海外貸出全体に占めるデータセンター向けの
割合は 3％、海外ファンド向けは9％だった。また、3メガ行の海外ファンド向け貸出残高は 12兆
円と、前年同月末から29.2％増加した。海外貸出全体に占める割合は現時点で限定的だが、収益
性の高さなどから注力分野となっている。データセンター事業者には賃料収入を特定の巨大IT企
業に依存するケースがあり、AI投資が期待どおりの収益を生まなければ、融資の返済能力にも影
響しかねない。また、技術革新によって高性能半導体などの設備が短期間で陳腐化するリスクも
ある。 
海外ファンド向け融資も注視すべきだろう。銀行はファンドに直接融資しているほか、ファン
ドが投資する企業や事業にも資金を供給している。ファンド向け貸出は、信用力の高い投資家の
出資約束を担保とする形式が多く、投資適格の比率は高い。ただ、日銀は、海外ファンドの運用
実態や資産価値を適時に把握するのは必ずしも容易ではないと指摘する。市場で大きなショック
が生じれば、ファンドが契約済みの融資枠から資金を引き出し、銀行の資金供給負担が増える可

### Page 4

4/8 
能性もある。

（2） 金融市場を通じた経路 
日本株の上昇は、AI関連需要の恩恵が相対的に大きい電機、精密、非鉄、機械などの業種がけ
ん引してきた。日銀は、AI関連投資に見合った収益拡大が実現しない場合、日本株にも調整圧力
が生じ得ると指摘しており、相応の株式を保有する金融機関にとってもリスクとなる。 
さらに、日銀によると、世界の株式市場に投資するヘッジファンドでは、AI関連銘柄の保有割
合が高まっている。マクロ戦略を採るヘッジファンドでは、個別株式の保有額に占める米大手ハ
イテク 7社の割合が、2019年の約14％から 2026年には約23％に上昇した（注 3）。こうしたファ
ンドのなかには、借り入れやデリバティブを活用して投資規模を拡大し、株式だけでなく国債な
どにも投資するものがある。AI関連株が急落すれば、損失の拡大や追加の証拠金請求などを受け
て、株式以外の投資ポジションも縮小する可能性がある。 
この影響が及び得る市場の一つが、日本の国債市場だ。ヘッジファンドを含む海外投資家は、
日本国債の現物と先物、金利スワップなどの価格差を利用した裁定取引を拡大させているとみら
れる。日銀によると、国債現物の対顧客取引に占める海外投資家の割合は、2015年の約 3割から
2025年には6割強に上昇した（注 4）。  
AI関連株の下落をきっかけにファンドがレバレッジを縮小すれば、国債関連のポジション解消
が進み、債券価格や金利の変動につながる可能性がある。日銀は、海外投資家による取引の増加
が市場の流動性や価格形成に寄与する場合がある一方、海外市場のショックが証拠金の請求など
をきっかけにポジション解消を引き起こし、日本の金融市場に影響を与えやすくなっている可能
性を指摘している。

（3） 実体経済を通じた経路 
日銀はストレステストで、ハイパースケーラーなどのAI関連投資が失速した場合、AI関連産業
の比重が高いアジア経済が大きく減速すると想定している。日本でも、情報通信機械、はん用・
生産用機械、電気機械などのAI関連産業を中心に経済が減速する。企業収益の悪化は、アジア向
けを中心とする貸出の信用コスト増加を通じて、金融機関に跳ね返る。 
AI関連投資を巡る三つの波及経路は相互に関連している。設備投資の減速による企業収益の悪
化は、株価の下落や信用リスクの上昇にもつながり得る。日本と海外NBFIの結びつきは、すでに
相当な規模に達している。日本の銀行による海外NBFIへの投融資残高は、2008年 3月末の8兆円
から 2026年6月末には126兆円に増加した。海外の投資ファンド等による日本の株式・債券への
投資残高も、同期間に111兆円から 408兆円へ拡大している（資料 2）。 このうち株式が 355兆円
を占め、債券は53兆円にとどまる（注5）。いずれもAI関連に限った金額ではないが、海外の金融
市場で発生したショックが日本に波及する経路の広がりを示している。

### Page 5

5/8

資料 2 日本の金融部門と海外ノンバンクの結びつき

（注）左図は国内銀行等による海外ノンバンクへの投融資残高。右図は、日銀がIMFの部門別対外証券投資データなどを用いて
試算した、海外主体による日本向け証券投資残高のうち「投資ファンド等」の値で、ディーラー・ブローカーやファイナンス会
社などを含む。海外ノンバンク全体の日本向け証券投資残高は 507兆円（2026年 6月末）。 
（出所）日本銀行「金融システムレポート」（2026年 10月号）図表Ⅴ-2-2（原資料はBIS、IMF、U.S. Department of the Treasury、
日本銀行）をもとに第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

4. 複合ショックにも耐性を示す日本の金融機関 
前章で整理した銀行融資、金融市場、実体経済の三つの波及経路に沿って、日本の金融機関へ
の影響をみていこう。日銀は銀行105行、信用金庫 247庫を対象に、2028年度末までの財務状況
を試算するマクロ・ストレステストを実施した。本稿で確認する「海外金利上昇シナリオplus」
では、AI関連投資の収益性に対する期待の縮小に加え、内外のNBFIによる資産売却などを通じて
金融市場の混乱が増幅される状況を想定した。 
まず、銀行融資を通じた波及経路を確認する。海外のデータセンター事業者やプライベートク
レジット（PC）ファンド向けの貸出が傷むというシナリオを置いた。両者に対する貸出の信用コ
スト率が最大約10ポイント悪化する想定だ。もっとも、国際統一基準行の信用コスト増加に占め
る海外ファンド・データセンター向けの寄与は限定的だった。 
金融市場を通じた経路では、株価や債券価格の大幅な下落を想定した。米国ではAI関連銘柄に
ITバブル崩壊後の下落率を当てはめるなどして、米国株価がベースラインから最大約 50％下落す
るとした。日本株も最大約 40％の下落を見込む。また、NBFIによるショックの増幅を織り込み、
米国のBBB格社債の信用スプレッドは最大約 1,100ベーシスポイント拡大する。日米の長期金利も
ベースラインより1.5ポイント上昇する。こうした株価と債券価格の同時下落によって、金融機
関の有価証券評価損益などが悪化する。また、海外金利の上昇に伴う外貨調達コストの増加が、
海外の資金利益を押し下げる。 
実体経済を通じた経路では、ハイパースケーラーなどのAI関連投資の失速によって、関連産業

### Page 6

6/8 
の比重が高いアジア経済が大きく減速すると想定した。日銀の試算では、アジアの実質GDPはベー
スラインから最大約7％下振れする。日本でも関連製造業を中心に企業収益が悪化し、銀行の信用
コスト増加につながる。今回のシナリオには原油高などの影響も含まれており、信用コストの増
加には国内向け貸出の寄与が大きい。 
こうした複合的なショックを想定しても、日銀の試算では金融機関の自己資本比率は全体とし
て規制水準を上回った。2028年度末の国際統一基準行の自己資本比率は、ベースラインの 12.8％
から 8.9％に低下する（資料 3）。

資料 3 マクロ・ストレステストにおける 2028年度末の自己資本比率

（注）対象は銀行 105行と信用金庫 247庫。「金融調整シナリオ」は、内外の金融市場や実体経済にリーマンショック並みの大幅
な調整が生じる想定。「海外金利上昇シナリオplus」は、内外金利の上昇、原油価格の上昇、AI関連投資の収益性に対する
期待の縮小などが同時に生じ、ノンバンク部門によるショックの増幅も織り込んだ想定。 
（出所）日本銀行「金融システムレポート」（2026年 10月号）図表Ⅵ-2-5、Ⅵ-2-7をもとに第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

この水準はリーマンショック並みの市場調整を想定した「金融調整シナリオ」の 10.1％を下回
る。今回のシナリオの影響の大きさが分かる。一方、国内基準行の銀行は 10.4％から 8.8％、信
用金庫は14.0％から 13.2％への低下にとどまった。国内基準行では、金融調整シナリオ（銀行
8.6％、信用金庫12.6％）の方が低下幅は大きい。 
日銀は、AI関連投資への期待の縮小などがNBFIを通じて増幅される複合的なショックに対して
も、「金融システムの安定性は全体として維持される」と評価している。ただ、試算結果は業態ご
との平均値で、個々の金融機関の自己資本比率にはばらつきがある点には留意が必要だろう。

5. おわりに 
日銀のストレステストでは、AI関連投資への期待の縮小やNBFIを通じたショックの増幅を織り
込んでも、日本の金融機関は全体として相応の耐性を備えているとの結果となった。一方、AI関

### Page 7

7/8 
連企業間の循環的な取引が、損失を増幅させる可能性にも注目したい。 
現在のAI関連企業の資金調達の特徴として、資金と売上が企業間で相互に結びつく関係があげ
られる。この循環的な取引を拙稿「AIと金融市場、強まる相互依存～IMFの国際金融安定性報告書
から読み解く新たなリスク～」で指摘した。本稿の 2章でも述べた通り、大手企業が財務基盤の
相対的に弱いAI企業に出資し、出資先が見返りとして大手企業のサービスや半導体を将来購入す
ることを約束する仕組みだ。IMFのGFSRによると、約 6,500億ドルの債務のうち約3,200億ドルは
出資先が負っており、そこには出資元の保証付きの債務やオフバランスの債務も含まれる。出資
先の経営が悪化すれば、出資元の将来の売上高が減少すると同時に、出資や融資の価値も損なわ
れる可能性がある。IMFはこれを「誤方向リスク（wrong-way risk）」と呼び、循環的な取引が損
失を増幅するリスクを評価するよう監督当局に求めている。 
筆者は、日本の金融機関もこの構造と無縁ではないと考える。データセンター事業者は、ハイ
パースケーラーとの長期リース契約を裏付けに、銀行などから資金を調達している。IMFによる
と、循環的な取引の出資を主導しているのはハイパースケーラーだ。出資先のAI企業の経営が悪
化すれば、取引相手となるハイパースケーラーの将来の売上高が下振れし、データセンターの需
要見通しにも影響が及ぶ可能性がある。そうなれば、ハイパースケーラーとのリース契約を裏付
けとする日本の大手行のデータセンター向け融資も、間接的に影響を受ける可能性がある。表面
上は信用力の高いハイパースケーラーとの契約に見えても、その先に循環的な取引が組み込まれ
ていれば、リスクは見かけより高いのではないか。 
日本の金融機関のデータセンター向けやファンド向けの貸出は、現時点では海外貸出の一部に
とどまる。それでも、損失が特定の企業群にとどまらず、連鎖的に広がっていくリスクには目を
配っておきたい。AI関連投資が持続的な収益を生み出せるかに加え、企業間の出資と取引の結び
つきが、銀行やNBFIの資金供給を通じて金融システムにどう波及するのか。その把握には、個々
の融資先の信用力だけでなく、取引の連鎖全体を見る視点が必要になるだろう。

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
We reaffirm our expectation that the RBA Monetary Policy
Board will again increase the cash rate at the November
meeting. Today’s inflation data and Senate testimony are
the final pieces of information before the RBA Monetary
Policy Board (MPB) meets next week. Recent data and

communication have consistently pointed to the staff
recommending an increase in the cash rate. We expect a
majority of the external members to agree, though some
members might dissent.
Inflation is above target, and above what the RBA’s August
forecasts implied. Some upside risks identified in August have
partially materialised, particularly energy prices. The RBA is
especially worried that above-target inflation will become
embedded in people’s expectations and pricing decisions. It

will therefore deem that further policy tightening is needed.
The RBA has made it clear that it is down-weighting the recent
easing in the labour market, even though this was more than it
forecast. In today’s opening statement to the Senate hearing,
the Governor said, “Despite the unemployment rate recently
rising a little more than we had expected, the labour market
seems a little tighter than we had previously judged. The
evidence for this is elevated underlying inflationary pressures.

That is, when we exclude some of the large volatile price
movements such as fuel we see inflationary pressure in areas
that reflect domestic demand for goods and services.” In the
Senate, the Governor also said that they were not worried
about the current pace of increase in unemployment.
Of course, this takes no account of the lagged relationship
between the labour market and inflation. The argument is that
if that measure of inflation last quarter was too high, it must

therefore be that the labour market is too tight. Nor does it
allow sufficiently for non-labour cost shocks, which business
surveys indicate are the real issue. But whether this judgement
by the RBA turns out to be right or not will not be revealed
for some time, certainly not soon enough to change its mind
before next week. And while some MPB members might have
a more optimistic view about labour supply trends than the
RBA staff, they are likely to be a minority.

The RBA is also relatively sanguine about the current
downturn in the housing market. The Governor and other
RBA officials are at pains to highlight that they do not target
housing prices; nor should they. The RBA considers that most,
though not all, of the current downswing is an expected effect
of tighter monetary policy.
RBA to hike in November, as inflation remains too high
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
The question then turns to the future beyond November. While

we still expect a hawkish tone in the RBA’s communication
until inflation has clearly turned down, further hikes beyond
next week are not currently our base case. Clearly, though, the
risks of another hike – most likely in February – have increased.
Our current forecast for Q4 inflation would seem to give them
cover for such a move. We note, however, that home-building
costs in the CPI have undershot expectations in the past couple
of months. This might imply a different view of the extent of

domestic demand pressures than the RBA currently believes.
However, a lot can happen in three months, as we have just
seen. The conflict in the Middle East could escalate further,
lifting energy prices beyond our current base case. It could
also go the other way, though, if the US and Iran manage to
strike a post-midterm-election deal. And as their counterparts
in New Zealand highlighted today, one risk is that the global
economy weakens suddenly. In the post-meeting statement,

the RBNZ Monetary Policy Committee said, “the world
economy could tip abruptly into a downturn causing weaker
inflation over the medium term.” This risk was something the
RBNZ MPC thought could crystallise in the next six weeks.
Whether on this timetable or something more drawn out, this
possibility does not appear to be on the RBA’s radar. Nor is it
our base case, though we have previously worried that the AI
financing boom could hit a wall quite suddenly.

“We reaffirm our expectation that the RBA
Monetary Policy Board will again increase the
cash rate at the November meeting"

THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: the source and persistence of inflation
will remain attentive to any broadening in inflation and related
risks through 2027; although, in the absence of another shock,
we expect them to remain on hold.
The Q3 GDP report certainly supported Chair Warsh’s view

on the breadth of growth, the 3.4% annualised gain for GDP
benefitting from a 3.8% annualised increase in household
consumption, 12.7% growth in non-residential investment
as well as a robust increase in government spending.
Notably though, the contribution from business investment
was effectively offset by net exports’ drag given most of
the componentry related to AI infrastructure investment is
imported. This is not to say that US firms won’t benefit from

the investment once complete, but simply that much of the
initial return goes to the foreign businesses who provide the
necessary capital equipment.
On consumption, the monthly PCE data implies downside risks
remain for the consumer, the increase in nominal spending
seen in the month requiring dissaving and, on a real basis,
consumption flat in the month following a 0.5% gain in
August. Cost-of-living pressures and uncertainty over the
outlook is expected to see household consumption growth

average around trend over the coming year, albeit with
considerable volatility quarter to quarter. This should see PCE
inflation slow back towards its 2.0%yr medium-term target as
the FOMC desires, from 3.6%yr and 3.1%yr for the headline
and core measures respectively as of September.
Across the Atlantic, the ECB also left its key policy
rates unchanged in October. Of note from the meeting
communications, energy prices continue to pose upside risks

for inflation, but also downside risks for growth. "While the
full effects of the energy shock are still unfolding, underlying
inflation has remained broadly stable so far and wages do not
show a material response". "Financing conditions have [also]
tightened", "which is likely to weigh on inflation and growth".
In the press conference, President Lagarde noted that,
between the October and December meetings, a significant
amount of new economic information will be released,

allowing forecasts and risks assessments to be tested.
Based on the October meeting communications, we continue
to expect a rate hike at the December meeting but believe this
will be prove to be the last hike of the cycle. Note though, the
spectrum of risks Europe faces is broad and skewed towards
inflation in the near term, hence the Council’s guidance will
remain hawkish. President Lagarde also announced today that
she will step down as ECB President in February ahead of the

scheduled conclusion of her term next October.
In Australia this week, all eyes were on the September CPI.
Headline inflation rose 0.8% (4.3%yr) in the month, largely
owing to a 9.4% jump in fuel prices and a seasonal surge in
domestic and international holiday travel & accommodation.
The underlying inflation pulse also proved hotter than
anticipated for Q3 overall, up 1.0% (3.7%yr).
While some parts of the basket are seeing disinflation –
housing in particular looks to be cooling faster than expected

as builders lure sales with discounts – other components are
still showing persistence. Highlighting this is market services
ex volatiles, which the RBA watches closely as an indicator
of price pressures related to domestic capacity. In Q3, this
metric rose 1.2% (3.2%yr), and the monthly detail points
to momentum holding up – in line with our expectations for
another chunky read in Q4.
With inflation running well above the RBA’s August forecasts,

and some of the energy risks flagged then materialising, we
continue to expect the Monetary Policy Board will deliver a 25bp
rate hike to 4.85% in November. Beyond November, another rate
hike in February is a risk, but is not currently part of our base case.
Across the Tasman, the RBNZ this week surprised market
expectations for a 25bp rate hike, deciding instead to wait for
further information before taking the next step with policy. The
decision was made 4:3, with the Governor in the ‘on hold’ camp. A

25bp hike in December to 3.00% and further tightening in 2027
(Westpac NZ forecasts a peak of 4.00% in September) seems
probable; however, each decision will depend on fuel prices,
inflation expectations and evidence of second-round pricing
pressures, along with the evolution of global economic risks.
In the US, the FOMC also held rates steady at its October
meeting. Based on prior comments by key members, the
decision was not a surprise, and neither were Governor Cook

and Cleveland Fed President Hammack’s dissents in favour
of a 25bp hike. The Committee remains positive on economic
activity, characterising it in the statement as “expanding at
a strong pace” (an upgrade from “solid” in September). Chair
Warsh also subsequently emphasised the breadth of economic
growth outside of tech-related investment (evinced by Q3
GDP the day after; see below).
The Committee refrained from spotlighting key price risks in
the statement. However, inflation “remains elevated” and the

FOMC are determined to achieve a “timely” and sustainable
return of annual inflation to 2.0%yr after 6 years above target.
This guidance and the “accumulation of more information will
help the Committee determine the policy stance” suggests
another 25bp hike is likely in December. Thereafter, the FOMC
Elliot Clarke, Head of International Economics
Ryan Wells, Economist
Illiana Jain, Economist

4
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
A lesson to be learned
The RBNZ delivered the biggest shock to markets outside of
a crisis period in over 20 years this week by leaving the OCR
unchanged. Markets had priced around a 98% probability of
a 25bp increase, and all major domestic analysts concurred.

After the CPI data the week earlier, we belatedly shifted our
call to a hike despite plenty of good arguments in favour of
the RBNZ sticking to its plan to pause. With the benefit of
hindsight, too much weight was put on market pricing and
short-term inflation drivers and not enough on the medium
term. That’s an important lesson to be learned.
The highlight of an action-packed week of central banking news
was the unexpected decision of the RBNZ to leave the OCR at

2.75%, despite near unanimous analyst expectations and market
pricing in favour of a 25bp hike. We had shifted sides from a
hold to a hike just a week earlier, in the wake of a CPI outcome
that was weaker than RBNZ expectations on the surface, but
with some evidence of firm core inflation undertones.
While we clearly cannot claim to have gotten this call right,
we always thought that the outcome was not the “done deal”
that markets had priced in. For example, in our preview of the

meeting we noted:
“We still think a more measured tightening approach is
appropriate from here. Raising the OCR at every meeting
until something ‘breaks’ could be a risky strategy if downside
growth concerns crystalise.”
And we saw a vote on the decision as very possible:
“A vote seems very possible as differences in view will likely
remain among the MPC about how much the medium-term
inflation outlook has really shifted in recent weeks.”
The 4:3 vote that was ultimately required to bring the MPC

to a decision is a testament to just how finely balanced the
decision was. We had thought that the hike was perhaps
around a 60-70% probability whereas the Record of Meeting
implies something more like a 50:50 call.
In the final analysis, the RBNZ has merely followed through on
the pause it signalled for this meeting back in the September
Monetary Policy Statement. All MPC members continue to see
a case for a higher OCR in time, but there are different views

around the timing of those increases. Governor Breman noted
that further interest rate increases remain likely in coming
meetings but are conditional on the whether the outlook
evolves in line with expectations. Indeed, the Governor noted
in later comments to press that a December hike was a “high”
probability and that the outlook remains for OCR “hikes” as the
base case. This suggests that the RBNZ’s view on the terminal
OCR hasn’t really shifted down from the 3.25% level presented

back in September. And it may be reasonable to speculate,
subject to data evolving as the RBNZ expects, that a modest
upgrade in those forward OCR forecasts could be in the offing.
So how did we all get this so wrong? This outcome was a real
shock given the historical record shows that over the history of
the OCR the market has without exception correctly predicted
a 25bp hike when the implied probability built into markets
was as high as it was. Clearly some recalibration of the model

is required. We can identify two key areas where lessons
should be learned: too much weight on market pricing; and not
enough on drivers of medium-term inflation.
Market pricing did not adequately reflect the
MPC’s desire to shift strategy.
Firstly, and most importantly, we put too much weight on
market pricing and too little on the RBNZ’s clearly stated
strategy of preferring a pause at the October meeting to
assess the impact of interest rate increases already done. In our

Preview we incorrectly surmised that silence equated to assent
and that market pricing of a hike would make an OCR hike the
less disruptive option – especially given the sense that a hike
to 3% was still likely in December as implied by the RBNZ’s
September forecasts. From a macroeconomic perspective,
raising rates six weeks early is of little consequence.
This logic, as sound as it was, was wrong, as market pricing did
not reflect a large enough change in the medium-term inflation

outlook to justify a change in RBNZ strategy. Markets at least
partially held the view, based on a long history of revealed
RBNZ preference, that the RBNZ keeps moving the OCR in the
same direction until confronted with compelling evidence that
a halt to the cycle is in prospect. That operating approach may
be under review now.
It’s not uncommon for other central banks to take breaks
while amid an interest rate cycle to gather information on
the impact of work already done before moving forward.

This week’s pause by the FOMC in the face of a fierce set of
monthly inflation readings in the last month is an example.
Pausing does not need to imply stopping or reversing. Indeed,
taking a more measured approach could prove to be solid
risk management – as we identified in our MPR preview note.
Risk management concerns probably should have greater
prominence in this cycle given we are seeing a measure of
Kelly Eckhold
Chief Economist NZ

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
5
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
supply side cost shocks that might not be persistent. The
Statement of Record contains a wealth of material related to
these sorts of uncertainties and concerns. Markets were not
listening to these concerns, however.
An insufficient focus on drivers of medium-term

inflation
Markets were not listening to the alarm bells because there
was a heavy focus on factors that may be more relevant to the
short-term inflation outlook than medium-term inflation. Top
of mind were the rise in energy prices and fall in the exchange
rate. Both factors look set to significantly boost the short-
term inflation outlook – for example we currently forecast
December quarter annual inflation of 4.3%, with upside risks
from there if fuel prices do not fall over the balance of the

quarter. However, our analysis of the data surprises suggested
no significant shift to the implied RBNZ OCR track one year
ahead. Upward pressure on the future OCR projection from
the lower exchange rate and stronger GDP was balanced
by higher long term interest rates and the lower Q3 CPI
outcome. Higher energy prices were not seen as a key factor
as they don’t impact medium term inflation unless inflation
expectations are dislodged (no evidence was found of that

since September) or if the increase in energy prices is viewed
as persistent (futures markets continue to imply lower forward
energy prices, although MPC hawks clearly see risks of
persistent inflation coming from supply shocks).
The RBNZ’s communications at the MPR and in its aftermath
are directing markets to pay more attention to medium-term
inflation drivers in the future. Hence the references to the
strength of demand and inflation expectations. The risk to

the RBNZ’s inflation forecasts are to the upside, and hence
we might reasonably expect some upgrade of the OCR profile
when we see new forecasts in December. But it’s not a done
deal by any means as data and events between now and then
will dictate what stance the RBNZ takes. This week’s business
and consumer confidence reports do not seem to present many
smoking guns of imminent second-round inflation pressures,
even though business and consumer sentiment seems to be

taking the higher oil price in its stride.
Is core inflation high enough yet to shift the
strategy?
Another interesting point made by the Governor was that the
markets (and analysts) misinterpreted how the September
quarter CPI was read by the RBNZ, which evidently took
greater comfort from the outcome. Governor Breman has noted
she wished the RBNZ has protocols in place to communicate
with markets the RBNZ’s view on that CPI data given that it
seems to have been a factor that figured importantly in at least

some MPC member’s thinking. Headline inflation was lower
than the RBNZ expected and importantly the core inflation
measures, that we had thought might have been of concern to
the MPC, do not yet seem to have been at a level sufficient to
shift the RBNZ from its pause-then-hike strategy. We remain
sceptical that a 3.25% OCR will prove sufficient to bring core
inflation down significantly. But core inflation appears to need
to lift for the MPC to become more agitated. It likely will lift,

but we won’t get more data on that until January 2027.
December hike likely but not a done deal
We expect a further 25bp hike at this year’s final meeting on 9
December and then 25bp increases in the February and March
2027 meetings, followed by a slower pace of increases at the
June and September Monetary Policy Statement meetings.
Our forecast of a peak OCR of 4% from September 2027 has
remained unchanged throughout, as has our assessment of a
3.75% neutral OCR.

That said, in the current uncertain environment, it goes without
saying that the evolution of monetary policy will depend on
how both global events and key data evolve. Therefore, as the
RBNZ noted itself, the exact timing of the tightening profile
is highly uncertain and even the tightening we forecast at the
December meeting should not be regarded as a done deal.
Looking ahead, the ‘hardest’ data to come will be the Q3
labour market surveys where we anticipate a small fall in

the unemployment rate to 5.5% but still moderate wage
pressures. The December quarter RBNZ inflation expectations
surveys will be of significant interest (17, 20 and 24
November) for MPC members looking for persistent inflation
impacts. The usual run of monthly activity and selected prices
indicators will be useful in narrowing down Q4 growth and
inflation momentum. And finally, the MPC’s focus on external
risks will imply keeping an eye on global fixed income, equity

and credit markets for signs of imminent calamity.
Finally, November 7 is the general election. A close race is
envisaged that could impact on the RBNZ’s mandate. We
will be offering client briefings on Monday 9 November and
Tuesday 10 November to outline the immediate implications
of the election outcome for markets and the economy. Contact
your relationship manager for details.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUS: RBA Policy Decision (%)
Nov 3, Last: 4.60, Westpac f/c: 4.85
Market f/c: 4.85, Range: 4.60 to 4.85
We reaffirm our expectation that the RBA Monetary Policy
Board (MPB) will decide to raise the cash rate by 25bps to
4.85% at the November meeting.

Inflation is above target, and above what the RBA’s August
forecasts implied. Some upside risks identified in August have
partially materialised, particularly energy prices. The RBA is
especially worried that above-target inflation will become
embedded in people’s expectations and pricing decisions.
Recent data and communication have consistently pointed to
the RBA staff recommending an increase in the cash rate. We
expect a majority of the external board members to agree,

though some members might dissent.
AUS: Oct Cotality Home Value Index (%mth)
Nov 2, Last: –1.1, Westpac f/c: –1.3
The Cotality home value index – covering all of Australia – fell
1.1% in Sep with downward revisions shaving a further 0.4%
off prices. The correction has now seen prices decline 5.2%
from their March peak, annual growth stalling flat. Cotality
reported 97% of capital city suburbs experienced falls over the
three months to September.
The October update is set to be another weak one with an

added hit coming from the RBA’s interest rate rise in late
September. The daily measure (covering the five major capital
cities rather than all of Australia) is tracking a 1.5% decline vs
1.3% in September. The detail points to slightly bigger falls
in Sydney, Brisbane and Perth with Melbourne continuing
to record more moderate declines. Note that seasonality is
usually a slight positive in the October month.
AUS: Sep Dwelling Approvals (%mth)
Nov 2, Last: –6.1, Westpac f/c: –7.0

Market f/c: 2.0, Range: –7.0 to 5.0
Dwelling approvals fell 6.1%mth in August, with the annual
pace easing slightly to 10.3%yr. Weakness centred on unit
approvals with private detached house approvals actually
posting a solid rebound from a July month fall, touching a new
cycle high (up 18.4%yr).
We expect the September update to show a much clearer
weakening. New construction is coming under pressure
from multiple fronts: interest rate rises; a sharp downturn in

established markets; and rising costs – with a great deal of
uncertainty around each of these. New home sales have fell
sharply through the September quarter and we expect to
see detached house approvals show something similar. Unit
approvals are coming off a weak read and should be more
stable. Total approvals are expected to print 7% lower.

### Page 7

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.7
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUS: Sep Goods Trade Balance ($bn)
Nov 5, Last: 0.5, Westpac f/c: 2.0
Market f/c: 1.0, Range: –1.0 to 2.5
Australia’s goods trade surplus narrowed to $0.5bn in August.
Recently, the trade balance has been oscillating between
small deficits and surpluses with significant volatility in the

underlying trade mix.
Swings in non-monetary gold, fuel and cars have all played
a large role, but the most eye-catching result from Aug was
the 79% surge in automatic data processing equipment (ADP)
imports, which captures the investment upswing related to
data centres.
For September, we expect an increase in the surplus as the
ADP import surge unwinds, though we are likely in store for
ongoing monthly volatility in trade for some time.
NZ: Sep Building Consents (%mth)

Nov 3, Last: +5.6%, Westpac f/c: Flat
Annual residential building consent numbers are currently at
a three year high, and we expect that they will remain firm in
the September update. However, monthly consent numbers
are showing signs of peaking, with falls in three of the last four
months. We’re watching to see if that trend has continued,
particularly given the ongoing softness in the housing
market.  In the non-residential space, we’ve seen softness

in the number of retail and office developments, but greater
resilience in the industrial and storage segment.
AUS: Sep Household Spending Indicator (%mth)
Nov 4, Last: flat, Westpac f/c: –0.5
Market f/c: 0.2, Range: –0.5 to 0.8
The household spending indicator was unchanged in August,
annual growth easing to 6.8%yr. The detail showed a modest
rotation away from discretionary categories towards non-
discretionary.
Our Westpac-DataX Card Tracker showed a decent rise in

the September month although new vehicle sales (which are
not covered by the tracker but are in scope for the spending
indicator) look to have posted a decline. Overall we expect
the indicator to show a 0.5% decline in the September month.
Note that the update with also include Q3 estimates of real
spending – we expect this to show a 0.6%qtr gain with prices
up 1.2%qtr and nominal spend up 1.8%qtr.

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.8
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
NZ: Q3 Labour Market Surveys (%)
Nov 4, Unemployment Rate, Last: 5.6, Westpac f/c: 5.5
LCI Private Sector, Last: 0.7, Westpac f/c: 0.5
We expect the unemployment rate to edge down to 5.5%
for the September quarter. Job numbers have been gaining
momentum in recent months, and now look to be enough to

outstrip population growth. However, the existing slack in the
labour market is likely to be keeping wage growth subdued,
despite cost-of-living pressures on workers. Our forecasts are
a little more positive overall than what the RBNZ assumed in
its September MPS forecasts.
US: Oct Employment Report
Nov 6, Payrolls, Last: 29k, Market f/c: 55k, WBC f/c: 75k
Nov 6, U/E rate, Last: 4.2%, Market f/c: 4.2%, WBC f/c: 4.3%
Nonfarm payrolls have shown considerable volatility of late,

July’s -10k followed by a 133k gain in August then a 29k
increase in September. However, the 3-month average of 51k
is likely a good guide on underlying momentum. In October, we
look for a 75k gain, although we expect some of that increase
to be offset by a modest reduction to the prior two months,
keeping the 3-month average around 50k.
While the participation rate has lifted in recent months, it
remains well below the average of 2025. Month-to-month, a

change either way is not guaranteed, but it seems likely that
the participation rate will trend higher in coming months. We
therefore expect the unemployment rate to edge up.

关注美联储加息计价上升可能。美国10月非农数据大超预期，新增16.5万人，且薪资增速超预期，美国10月核心
CPI环比上涨0.3%，高于市场预期的0.2%，整体CPI环比上涨0.4%，同样超出预期的0.3%。经济韧性的背景叠加油
价短期居高不下，通胀向其他领域传导或将更顺畅，中期选举后多为美联储票委发表鹰派讲话，需要警惕加息计

中东局势再度反复。随着共和党在中期选举上失利，美国进一步推进美伊和谈的意愿上升，但若伊朗方要价过高
和谈也较难推进，此前美伊双方谈判重点有所分歧，美方聚焦伊朗去核化，伊朗聚焦美国撤军。11月8日美国总统
特朗普在接受媒体采访时明确表示不打算升级与伊朗的战事，同时美方媒体还透露万斯过去两周积极推动新停火
并试图恢复核谈。11月11日，伊朗最高国家安全委员会秘书雷扎伊表示，伊朗不会让一点浓缩铀运出境。11月12
日，特朗普表示“不需要与伊朗达成协议 达成协议的时代早已过去”。随着特朗普的最新表态，美伊局势朝着长期
僵持局势发展的概率正持续上升。 
国内稳信心政策陆续出台。按美元计，中国10月出口同比增长41.6%，进口同比增35.2%，均高于预期。10月投资、
消费数据表现偏弱。10月20日发改委新闻发布会释放正在研究制定扩大内需战略实施方案，11月2日央行推出超
1000亿证券、基金、保险公司互换便利(SFISF)推动市场信心企稳。关注11月17-18日的APEC会议。 
分板块来看，关注农产品的机会。民主党全面掌控国会压制铜关税预期以及油价，短期利空铜价、利好黄金，远
端则需要关注加息预期的变化。能源方面，尽管美伊局势仍往僵持方向发展，但实际中东的原油出口量明显回升，
在不造成生产设施破坏的前提下，油品及化工将温和调整。农产品则关注厄尔尼诺的气候预期，以及对应减产影
响，10月份USDA报告出现产量不及预期的信号，农产品有望重新启动。

## Document 365: How the blue wave could reshape US energy and data centres

Source PDF: all report pdf/How the blue wave could reshape US energy and data centres.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-05
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Energy aﬀordability shaped the midterms. A divided
government will now face pressure to shield consumers from
rising data centre power costs while speeding up new energy
supply. Onshoring and trade protections will continue, despite
higher near-term costs, as energy security remains a priority
Coco Zhang
After the midterm elections, the government will need to minimise data
centre-related costs for consumers, bring new power supply online faster and

protect supply chains. Pictured: Data centre in Ohio
Exit polls from the US midterm elections highlighted a key concern
shaping voters’ decisions: energy aﬀordability. Nearly 90% of
voters said the cost of living was a crucial or major factor in their
vote for Congress, with many pointing to gasoline and utility costs.
Meanwhile, as the AI data centre boom strains power supply and
infrastructure, 56% said the issue was a crucial or major factor in

their vote.
With Democrats now set to control both the House and Senate,
what does this mean for US energy and data centre policy?

### Page 2

Newly elected lawmakers will face pressure to address these
concerns, requiring the Democratic Congress and Republican White
House to ﬁnd common ground on shielding consumers from data
centre-related costs and accelerating new power supply, including
through permitting reform. Onshoring and supply chain protections
are also likely to continue. While they can raise near-term costs,
they remain important to US energy security. Developments in all
three areas could have signiﬁcant implications for businesses.

That said, Congress needs to strike a balance when drafting
legislation aimed at achieving these goals. More aggressive
measures could face a veto from President Trump.
Strengthened data centre regulation ahead,
but the devil is in the detail
The data centre industry is facing heightened local opposition.
Recent polls suggest that around one in seven Americans is
concerned about or opposes data centre development in their
neighbourhoods.
In response to this issue, there is likely to be tighter data centre

regulation. State-level policy will be more important to watch, as
action is politically and practically easier at this level. The most
likely approach is targeted regulation, much of which is already
emerging. This includes data centre-related taxes, reduced tax
incentives, environmental standards, and requirements for
developers to bear relevant cost increases. Outright moratoriums
remain unlikely to become mainstream. Where they are introduced,

they are more likely to be temporary, giving states time to develop
a data centre policy framework.
At the federal level, the key unanswered question is how much
authority should remain with the states and whether federal
legislation should mandate state regulations or simply provide
guidance. Republicans generally favour letting states lead, while
Democrats tend to support a stronger federal role.
We think sweeping data centre legislation is unlikely because of

President Trump’s veto power. Federal action is more likely to focus
Your privacy is important

### Page 3

on aﬀordability by protecting electricity customers from higher
costs and formalising data centres' responsibility to help fund grid
and energy infrastructure upgrades. Such measures could be added
to broader legislation, such as must-pass spending bills needed to
avoid a government shutdown, or a bipartisan package like
permitting reform.
More broadly on AI, we expect the Democratic Party to increase
scrutiny of technology companies, as discussed here, while utilising

the next two years to prepare an AI governance framework for 2028
and beyond.
However, tighter AI and data centre regulation is unlikely to
fundamentally alter the industry’s growth trajectory. AI’s
importance to US economic growth and competition with China will
remain powerful drivers of expansion.
Business implications
State-level policy would more directly aﬀect project siting,
which is important for data centre companies to watch.
Where state rules are tighter, project development could slow.

However, a more restrictive regulatory environment could
also increase the value of data centres already approved or
under construction, easing some of the industry’s capital
constraints. Higher valuations could also encourage
developers to be more selective and expand at a more
measured pace, ultimately improving the quality of the
project pipeline.
Developers should also treat community engagement as a
strategic investment. Recent surveys show that while only

20-30% of Americans support local data centre construction,
support can rise to 50-70% when there are accompanying
community investments, such as in public infrastructure
improvements or reductions in residents' costs. More
developers are now signing binding community beneﬁt
agreements as part of project development.

Enabling faster power generation through
permitting reform
Both parties support timely expansion of electricity supply to meet

rising demand from AI and manufacturing reshoring. They also
generally agree that energy abundance can help reduce cost
pressures. While Democrats and Republicans diﬀer on the preferred
energy mix, there is still common ground. Technologies likely to
retain bipartisan support include nuclear, geothermal, carbon
capture, and biofuels.
However, the greatest potential for policy action may lie not in
speciﬁc technologies, but in permitting reform.
Today, many energy projects can spend over ﬁve years navigating

the federal permitting process. Around $240bn to $280bn of
infrastructure investment across the eight sectors below enters the

With both parties recognising the need for reform, the Senate
introduced the Bipartisan American Aﬀordability and Jobs Act
(BAAJA) in September 2026. Key permitting proposals include:

Speeding up approvals for energy projects
Providing greater certainty and consistency across
administrations
Making it easier to permit power transmission lines
Despite debate over its environmental impact, there is broad
agreement that the BAAJA would meaningfully improve the
permitting process. The key question is whether it can become law
under the current Congress. The Senate is expected to vote on 9
November, after which the bill would still need approval from the

House and President Trump. Democrats may want to delay the bill
and seek changes once they regain control of Congress next year,
but doing so could increase the risk of a presidential veto. This
creates an incentive to reach a deal sooner rather than later.

### Page 6

Even if the BAAJA does not pass under the current Congress,
permitting reform is likely to remain a bipartisan theme. The
growing need to expand power supply and infrastructure more
quickly will keep pressure on future Congresses to streamline
permitting.
Business implications
For energy companies – both renewables and fossil fuels –
the BAAJA could signiﬁcantly shorten permitting timelines. If
enacted, it would give project developers greater certainty,

reducing the risk of litigation and permit cancellations. This
could further expand project siting options for developers.
Nevertheless, in the very near term, projects may need to
retain ﬂexibility around their permitting strategy while the
new policy framework is developed and implemented.
Expect more protectionism and reshoring
efforts against China dominance
From clean energy technologies to the critical minerals that
underpin much of the US economy, China controls a large share of

global production capacity. Consequently, reducing reliance on
China will remain a US policy priority. Such eﬀorts would increase
rather than reduce project costs in the near term. But the long-term
beneﬁts of stronger domestic manufacturing and more secure
supply chains could outweigh those costs.
Global supply chain capacity distribution
across various sectors

Source: ING Research, BloombergNEF, International Energy Agency
This creates two key risks. First, heavy reliance on China leaves US
industries vulnerable to geopolitical tensions. We have already seen
this with rare earths, where Chinese export restrictions contributed
to disruptions for US auto manufacturers. Second, China’s supply
chain dominance can put US manufacturers at a competitive
disadvantage.
Building more secure energy and natural resource supply chains

has long attracted bipartisan support in the US. The Trump
administration has supported domestic rare earth production
through funding, purchase agreements, and equity investments. In
solar, China-focused trade and tax credit restrictions are also
supporting the expansion of domestic manufacturing.
We expect this focus to continue. The executive branch could
pursue further protective measures and is unlikely to face major
opposition from Congress. Congress could also authorise additional

funding to strengthen rare earth supply chains.
Furthermore, a Democratic-controlled Congress could push to
extend clean manufacturing tax credits as part of broader
negotiations with President Trump. Given the administration’s focus
on countering China and expanding US manufacturing, an
extension could emerge as a compromise in a broader legislative
package under an optimistic scenario. All these eﬀorts would also
extend into future administrations.

Business implications
For US companies:
Clean energy companies and other industries that rely on
rare earths, including semiconductors, AI, defence, and

electronics, should have enough policy certainty to build
long-term supplier relationships outside China. In the near
term, however, diversiﬁcation could raise supply chain costs
as alternative sources may be more expensive. Solar could
face particular cost pressures, as some tariﬀs on supply
chain equipment also apply to non-Chinese suppliers.
For overseas companies:
US eﬀorts to build supply chains outside China are creating
opportunities for manufacturers elsewhere. The US is

supporting rare earth production in Australia and Brazil, while
solar and battery suppliers in countries such as South Korea
and Japan could beneﬁt from stronger demand. This could
also encourage more manufacturing investment in the US.
Meanwhile, European clean energy developers could face
greater competition for non-Chinese supplies. While the EU
has not imposed US-style tariﬀs, it is tightening restrictions
on Chinese equipment through funding, procurement,

auction, and subsidy rules. This could intensify global
competition for non-Chinese suppliers and push costs higher.

### Page 4

◆ The economy remains on the recovery track despite weakness in some
areas
Annualized real GDP growth in the second quarter was +1.1% QoQ, with growth
staying in positive territory for three straight quarters. Although personal
consumption exhibited weakness and capital investment declined, overall growth
was lifted by foreign demand. Exports, particularly of services, increased, and the
drop in imports of Middle Eastern crude oil also helped foreign demand turn positive.

◆ Housing starts are weak
Housing starts surged 18.6% YoY in June. They have continued to rise on a YoY
basis, but the jump was mainly due to a rebound from the slump in the same month
of the previous year. The recovery in the number of starts is actually moderate,
reflecting higher interest rates and soaring construction costs.
◆ Earthquake impact will need to be watched
Damage from the earthquake that occurred in Kumamoto Prefecture at the end of

July is weighing on economic activity in the affected areas. Although moves toward
recovery can be seen, with many companies having already resumed operations,
the impact could become prolonged in the following scenarios:
The first is if damage to capital stock such as factories and roads has been severe.
Some companies have sustained damage to production facilities, making an early
resumption of operations difficult in some cases.
The second is if a downturn in tourism demand occurs. According to some media

reports, there has been an uptick in cancellations of accommodation reservations in
Kumamoto Prefecture and neighboring prefectures since the earthquake. After the
2016 Kumamoto earthquake, the decline in travel demand from Japanese visitors
was short lived, but lower demand from foreign tourists persisted for a long period.
Annualized real GDP growth was +1.1% quarter over quarter (QoQ) in Q2 2026

Monthly Report of Prospects for Japan's Economy August 2026

Exports and inbound demand remain solid despite weakness in some areas
◆ Goods exports are on the verge of picking up
Goods exports are treading water. A sharp rise in memory chip prices is among
factors pushing up export prices, resulting in record high levels of exports in value
terms recently. On the other hand, the increase in volume terms is limited. One

possible reason is that Japan's export competitiveness has declined in fields such as
semiconductors, where global demand is growing.
Looking ahead, exports are expected to pick up. Capital investment by chipmakers
is expanding worldwide, and this should lift exports of semiconductor manufacturing
equipment, an area in which Japan has strength. However, continued caution is
needed regarding the effects of prolonged turmoil in the Middle East. A global

slowdown due to elevated crude oil prices could weigh on exports.
◆ Reluctance to visit among Chinese travelers is weighing on demand for
travel to Japan
Inbound demand is currently soft. The Chinese government's advisory urging its
citizens to refrain from traveling to Japan is having an adverse impact. The number
of Chinese visitors to Japan has fallen to about half the level prior to the advisory.
Meanwhile, the number of tourists from countries other than China remains firm.

Inbound demand is expected to stay robust going forward. Continued increases in
tourists from countries other than China will remain a supporting factor. The
expectation that the yen will remain weak will also act to push up inbound
consumption. However, there are two downside risks going forward:
The first is that the Chinese government keeps the advisory in place for a
prolonged period. When China banned group travel to South Korea in 2017, tourist

numbers stayed low for two to three years.
The second is the impact of higher crude oil prices. Major airlines have embarked
on phased increases in fuel surcharges, which could weigh on inbound demand
during the summer.

### Page 6

Personal consumption is gradually recovering thanks to a favorable income environment
◆ Higher disposable incomes are supporting personal consumption
Personal consumption continues to gradually recover. While consumption of
non-durable goods remains weak, services consumption is firm, particularly in
amusement-related services. In addition, consumption of durable goods has
increased significantly. Temporary factors such as the abolition of the environmental

performance tax on automobiles (since April 2026) and the tightening of
energy-efficiency standards for air conditioners (effective April 2027) have also
contributed to this rise.
A favorable income environment can also be pointed to as a background factor
behind the recovery in personal consumption. YoY real disposable income growth has
generally been positive since mid-2024, and the pace of increase has recently
accelerated. Employee remuneration is rising, partly as a result of agreements made

during the shunto (spring wage negotiations), and net asset income is also increasing
at a faster clip as companies hike dividends in line with stock price rises.
◆ Stock price developments could be a source of concern for personal
consumption
Personal consumption is expected to continue its gradual recovery going forward. In
addition to the favorable income environment and government policy measures to
curb price increases, a recovery in consumer confidence driven by easing tensions in

the Middle East will also provide a tailwind. Recently, the Consumer Confidence Index
has been showing signs of bottoming out.
However, a reverse wealth effect stemming from falling stock prices is a potential
cause for concern. The Nikkei Stock Average, which reached a new high in June, has
been struggling to push higher since the start of July. Estimates suggest the wealth
effect is concentrated among elderly households, but in recent years working-age

households have also increased their holdings of equity-related assets, and the
positive wealth effect for them may have supported consumption to some extent. For
this reason, if stock prices were to fall significantly, there is a danger of downward
pressure on consumption across a wide range of households.

◆ The unemployment rate has settled in the mid-2% range
While the employment situation is generally favorable, weakness can be seen in
some areas. The unemployment rate has remained in the mid-2% range since 2022.
The number of employed persons in non-manufacturing, where business conditions
are favorable, is holding firm, while in manufacturing the number remains at a level
below that seen before the COVID-19 pandemic.
In the background to the drop in manufacturing employment is a slump in

production activity. Manufacturing output has been shrinking due to the effects of
U.S. tariff policy, soaring raw material prices, and a decline in the international
competitiveness of Japanese firms. As a result, pressure to reduce headcounts has
been building. Even in non-manufacturing, some industries closely linked to
manufacturing, such as transportation and wholesale/retail, are also exhibiting
weakness in labor demand.
◆ The employment situation could remain fragile in the near term

Looking ahead at the employment situation in the short term, a sense of
stagnation may persist. The contraction in output in the manufacturing sector will
continue to weigh on labor demand. Although production activity has been brisker
recently, particularly in semiconductor-related industries, the employment-creation
capacity of such industries is small. The employment-inducement coefficient per unit
of new demand tends to be lower than in other sectors, meaning that the industries

lack the strength to serve as drivers of employment.
However, in the medium to long term, the unemployment rate is expected to see a
gradual decline. Demand for labor is projected to stay solid, especially in
face-to-face services such as medical/nursing care and accommodation and
eating/drinking services, but the number of people seeking employment from the
non-labor force population has declined significantly due to the expansion in elderly
and female workforce participation. With limited room for expansion in the labor

supply, the job market is expected to tighten further.
